景気の底堅さとインフレ圧力の根強さを確認。貿易
赤字はAI関連を含む設備投資需要などを背景に拡
大。非製造業PMIは拡大圏で推移する一方、仕入
価格指数の上昇は企業のコスト負担増を示唆

ユーロ圏総合PMIは節目の50超を維持。小売売上
高も小幅増加。ドイツの鉱工業生産は持ち直したが、
製造業受注は大型案件の反動で減少。景気は緩
やかな回復を維持するも、持続力には不透明感
欧
州
ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き
注目点② 日銀さくらレポートは企業活動の底堅さを再確認注目点①米欧で金利上昇。異なる背景と金融政策への含意

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

0 10 20 30

金利 × 設備投資
価格転嫁×企業収益
AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

げに伴う販売数量の減少も

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に
 今次局面では、現状、主要拠点への直接被害は確認されておらず、一部工場の生産停止にとどまっている模様
―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

（注） 旅行収支要因は、非居住者による国内消費（インバウンド消費）を控除し、居住者に
よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

金利上昇の「質」が問われる日本
～「良い金利上昇」を実現するための4つの論点～
（2026年7月31日発刊）
調査部レポートのご紹介 PR
2026年8月作成
レポート
URL
レポート
二次元
コード
日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？
Q2. 先行きの金利上昇の「質」を左右するポイントは何か？
Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？
問われるのは「良い金利上昇」か
「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

### Page 1

M
Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Announces additional
investment in Malaysia
subsidiary
What’s new? Co-Tech’s board today approved an additional NT$1.4bn investment in
its 100%-owned subsidiary, CoTech Development (Penang) Sdn. Bhd., to be financed
with internal funds or bank loans. This follows the company’s September 14
announcement that it would establish a subsidiary in Southeast Asia (note).
Our view: Our latest checks suggest that, due to strong demand from key

customers, the Malaysia plant will primarily produce advanced HVLP copper foil
rather than the RG-series foil originally planned. The new plant is also likely to be
larger than Yunlin Plant III, where Co-Tech plans to add ~600 tons of HVLP3/4
capacity by end-2027. Volume production in Malaysia is unlikely to start before late
2028, so most of the ramp-up should fall in 2029, contributing to that year’s
earnings. On funding, our checks also suggest Co-Tech will finance further

investment in the new plant’s construction and equipment by issuing convertible
bonds.
See also our initiation report: Asia Pacific Technology: AI’s Hidden Backbone (13 Sep
2026)

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
CPO/NPO and micro     VCSEL based   optical solutions are the
focus for networking, while HVDC takes center stage for power.
Higher bandwidth and better power efficiency: In the networking space, the
migration into higher bandwidth continues, as players like Accton and Celestica
showcased their 1.6T network switches. We think a bigger theme is the effort to
improve the power efficiency of current mainstream pluggable optical solutions.

First off, one well-discussed approach is the adoption CPO or NPO, and it seems the
latter is gaining more attention due to the technical challenges in the former. Spec
standardization, such as CPX connectors, could further accelerate the proliferation,
in our view. Secondly, copper could still help, as Credo has its 1.6T AEC cables for
inter-tray transmission while many other vendors (Amphenol, TE Connectivity,
Molex, etc.) have their 448G CPC solutions for in-tray transmission. Lastly, the micro

VCSEL based optical modules from Credo and ALAB represent another power
efficient solution (12W/end at 1,6T), which has reliability comparable to copper
(100M hours of MTBF/MTBI) for a transmission distance of 50-100m. We believe
this micro VCSEL based solution could potentially replace the AEC for shorter
distance transmission when copper hits the physical limit in the medium term and
be integrated with the CPO architecture in the longer term. In sum, we believe the

above-mentioned solutions are likely to co-exist for different use cases.
800VDC for power delivery taking the center stage: While the Vera Rubin NVL72
rack already has the in-rack power shelf reference design (110KW/shelf at 3+1
redundancy), Nvidia unveiled another reference design this time, using one 660KW
800VDC power rack to support two Vera Rubin NVL72 racks. Moreover, we also
saw a few ±400VDC reference designs as well. Our conversations with companies

on the exhibition floor indicate that the HVDC adoption should start from late
2026, but likely at a small scale extending into 2027. The next checkpoint will be
Vera Rubin Ultra racks, where increasing power consumption and more hardware
devices for inter-rack scale-up network might take up more pace on the rack, though
the liquid cooled power shelf  should ease the space budget a bit. We believe both
solutions are progressing and will closely monitor the dynamics.

Stock picks: We believe that Bizlink (3665.TW) should be a beneficiary of the
proliferation of the micro VCSEL based optical solution, as it is the assembly partner
for Credo and potentially others as well. Chroma (2360.TW) should see drivers
from power testing, CPU/GPU/TPU SL T/FT, and photonics testing (pluggable/
CPO)tools. Delta( (2308.TW) and Lite-On (2301.TW) should continue to grow with
the increasing power consumption of AI clusters, but more meaningful growth

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

CPX and NPX all marching forward
Though CPO remains as the holy grail in the space, its yield issue and lower serviceability
still present challenges to the wider adoption in the near term. We believe that the efforts
will continue, but the industry is simultaneously pursuing other more practical solutions

to sort out the power consumption issue of the existing pluggable transceivers,  including
NPO (near-packaged optics), CPC (co-packaged copper) and NPC (near-packaged copper)
Compared to CPO, NPO compromises on the power consumption, but in exchange it eases
the stringent requirement on alignment and serviceability. Given its better technology
readiness and more mature supply chain, we think it might see good traction in the
coming years.

VCSEL based optical solutions gaining more attention
In addition to CPO/NPO/CPC/NPC designs, we see the micro VCSEL based optical
solutions are gaining more attention too. The value proposition is to leverage the wide
and slow architecture for a relatively short transmission distance of 50-100m. It can
achieve 1-2 pJ/bit of power consumption vs. 5-10pJ/bit for CPO and NPO architecture. The

current generation can enable 32Gbps of bandwidth per lane and the 100-200Gbps of
bandwidth per lane is in the roadmap.

In sum, different solutions of data transmission are emerging, trying to improve the power

Power becoming another bottleneck in the AI deployment
HVDC adoption emerging from Vera Rubin
In the power space, HVDC has been one of the key focuses in the AI cluster, as the rapid
increase in AI rack power density is creating growing challenges for conventional power-
distribution architectures. As rack-level power consumption rising from tens of kilowatts
to several hundreds of KWs, electrical losses, cable size, thermal management
requirements, and overall system complexity increase significantly. Under traditional low-

voltage power architectures, delivering larger amounts of power requires proportionally
higher current, which increases resistive losses and places greater stress on cables,
connectors, busbars, and power-conversion equipment.
At the same time, every power-conversion stage introduces efficiency losses and
generates additional heat. As AI deployments scale into multi-megawatt and eventually
gigawatt-level campuses, even small efficiency improvements can translate into

### Page 10

M
Idea
10
meaningful reductions in operating costs, cooling requirements, and infrastructure
investment. Consequently, the industry is increasingly focused on power architectures
that can reduce conversion stages, lower current levels, and improve overall power-
delivery efficiency.
Against this backdrop, high-voltage direct current (HVDC) is emerging as one of the most
important architectural upgrades for next-generation AI infrastructure. By increasing

distribution voltage, HVDC allows the same amount of power to be delivered at a lower
current, reducing conduction losses and improving system efficiency. Higher-voltage
architectures also improve scalability for ultra-high-density rack deployments by
alleviating some of the physical and thermal constraints associated with conventional
power-distribution systems.
Exhibit 18: Better power efficiency for 800VDC architecture
Source: Delta
Exhibit 19: Better efficiency leads to cost savings

Source: Delta
In a joint keynote speech by Delta and Nvidia, the roadmap illustrates the industry's
gradual migration toward higher-voltage power architectures. Earlier AI server generations
primarily utilized conventional power-delivery schemes centered around rack-level 48V
distribution. As rack power requirements increased with Blackwell-generation systems,
industry participants began evaluating higher-voltage architectures capable of supporting

substantially greater power density. Looking ahead, the industry is increasingly converging
around 800VDC as a potential next-generation standard for large-scale AI deployments,
particularly as rack-level power consumption approaches several hundred kilowatts. The
transition reflects the need to improve power-distribution efficiency while supporting the
continued growth of AI computing infrastructure.
Though we also saw the 800VDC power rack reference design for the Vera Rubin rack in a

1-to-2 configuration (one 660KW power rack supporting two Vera Rubin racks), we think
the adoption of this solution might be relatively low, since the Vera Rubin rack also has a
reference design for the in-rack power shelf design

### Page 11

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 11
Exhibit 20: 800VDC roadmap
Source: Delta
Exhibit 21: 800VDC reference design for VR
Source: Delta
Liquid cooled power shelf on the horizon
The next key check point to watch should be the Vera Rubin Ultra generation, as the
power consumption per rack shall continue to increase and there might be more hardware
devices to enable the inter-rack scale up network, both could take up more space on the
IT rack. That said, the power shelf will shift from air cooling into liquid cooling, which

could narrow the power shelf height by 1U to free up some space as well. Our checks
indicate that both the in-rack power shelf and 800VDC power rack solutions are ongoing
now, and we believe that rack space, power redundancy and system reliability are all the
factors being considered. We'll closely monitor developments on this front.
Exhibit 22: Liquid cooled power shelf
Source: Megmeet
Exhibit 23: Liquid cooled power shelf in rack
Source: Megmeet

Solid state transformers on the Nvidia RVL for the first time
In the MGX ecosystem board, where Nvidia usually list the recommenced vendor lists and
product samples, we saw SST this time. Delta, Heron. DG Matrax, Sungrow, Solaredge,
Megmeet, Luxshare, etc.

### Page 12

M
Idea
12
Exhibit 24: SST vendors on the Nvidia MGX ecosystem board
Source: Morgan Stanley
Looking further ahead, Solid-State Transformers (SSTs) represent a potential next-
generation evolution of power infrastructure. Unlike traditional transformers that operate
primarily as passive devices, SSTs leverage power electronics to provide voltage
conversion, power conditioning, and bidirectional energy management within a single
platform. This enables greater controllability and flexibility while supporting increasingly

digitalized power networks.
Although SSTs remain at an early stage of commercialization and face challenges related
to economics, reliability, and large-scale deployment, the technology aligns well with long-
term data center trends. As AI campuses evolve into utility-scale power consumers
incorporating energy storage, distributed generation, and advanced power management,
SSTs could eventually become a key enabling technology.

### Page 13

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 13
Therefore, while HVDC is expected to drive the next phase of power architecture
upgrades, SSTs represent a longer-term technology roadmap that could further transform
how future data centers distribute and manage electricity.

architecture

According to Infineon, SST could represent an emerging >$1bn market opportunity by
2030, as the technology begins to replace a portion of the >$15bn conventional
transformer market, particularly in smaller-power applications. While SST penetration is
expected to remain relatively modest versus the overall transformer market, the
opportunity is significant for relevant players in the longer term.

Lite-On Technology (2301.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 17x based on average 2027-28 EPS. We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 47% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.4, versus
the stock's 16x P/E and 2% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 7.2.

n Stronger optical business growth
n Stronger data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand growth

n Downtrend in raw material prices
Risks to Downside
n Macro demand weakness
n Weaker data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand slowdown
n Uptrend in raw material prices
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.

n Faster-than-expected global economic growth
n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 45x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of

~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 45x is justified by the 61% operating profit CAGR we project for
2025-28.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

Document 202: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

Document 228: AI数据中心泡沫将近？英伟达支持的Nscale估值接近腰斩，微软也撤了

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI数据中心泡沫将近？英伟达支持的Nscale估值接近腰斩，微软也撤了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

英国新云计算公司 Nscale 因电⼒接⼊延误遭微软撤单，且 IPO 估值被投资者要求降⾄ 160 亿
⾄ 190 亿美元，较原计划缩⽔超四成。这成为⼀个⽉内第五宗折戟的 AI 数据中⼼上市案，反
应出供电瓶颈、巨额亏损与⾼融资成本正重创 AI 基础设施泡沫。
摘要：
英伟达背书、坐拥千亿美元合同承诺的英国新云计算公司 Nscale ，正遭遇与⼀个⽉前澳⼤利亚
同⾏ Firmus 如出⼀辙的困境： IPO 估值被投资者要求接近腰斩，微软宣告撤出关键数据中⼼项
⽬。
据彭博报道， Nscale 此前寻求以约 300 亿美元估值上市，但已有⼀批投资者明确表⽰， 160 亿⾄
190 亿美元 " 更可能获得买家认可 "—— 较原定估值缩⽔逾四成。与此同时，据路透援引《电讯
报》的报道，微软已终⽌与 Nscale 位于英国埃塞克斯郡洛顿（ Loughton ）数据中⼼园区的合作
协议，原因是英国国家电⽹官员告知 Nscale ，该站点最早可能要到 2030 年代才能获得供电。微
软对此拒绝置评。

这是不到⼀个⽉内第五宗 AI 数据中⼼上市计划遭遇挫折的案例，前四宗分别涉及软银旗下 SB
Energy 、 Firmus 、菲律宾 PLDT ⼦公司 Vitro 及⿊⽯旗下 AirTrunk 。⾼盛信贷团队将这⼀系列事件
归结为 "AI 交易遭遇资⾦成本 " ，⽽信贷市场早在数周前已通过债券价格和信⽤违约互换的恶化率
先发出预警。
微软撤局：供电⽆期，锚定客⼾先⾏离场
洛顿项⽬的失败，反映出当前 AI 基础设施建设的核⼼⽭盾：合同可以⼀天之内签订，电⽹连接却
可能遥遥⽆期。
据路透报道，微软终⽌协议的直接导⽕索是国家电⽹的告知 —— 该站点直⾄ 2030 年代都⽆望接
⼊电⼒供应。 Nscale ⽅⾯回应称，与微软的 " 关系依然稳固且⻓期 " ，将此次问题定性为 " 伦敦范
围内的电⽹问题 " ，并表⽰正在 " 探索替代能源⽅案 " 。然⽽，锚定客⼾已然离场，这⼀表态难以
弥补实质性的商业损失。
微软今年已不⽌⼀次选择撤出第三⽅ AI 算⼒项⽬ —— 据彭博此前报道，今年 6 ⽉，微软曾拒绝了

⼀笔价值 30 亿美元的甲⻣⽂云计算合同。当全球现⾦储备最为充裕的超⼤规模云服务商开始对
租⽤算⼒挑剔起来，主打 " ⽐任何⼈更快上线算⼒ " 的新云计算公司的核⼼商业逻辑便受到直接挑
战。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

挪威旗舰园区的情况同样不容乐观。 Nscale 位于北极圈以北的格洛姆峡湾（ Glomfjord ）设施已
延误六个⽉，原因涵盖冷却系统泄漏、当地法规、建设延误及⽕灾事故。计划中 30 兆⽡的容
量，⽬前实际运⾏的仅有 6.4 兆⽡。
千亿合同下的真实账本： 4% 的落地率与 107 倍的估值泡沫
Nscale 的招股说明书在头部数字上颇为亮眼，但细究之下，数据显⽰出现实与承诺之间的巨⼤
鸿沟。
公司披露的合同总价值⾼达 1034 亿美元，较两年半前创⽴时的约 1 亿美元急剧扩张。然⽽实际在
运营的合同价值仅为 26 亿美元；已上线容量为 55 兆⽡（其中 48 兆⽡为租⽤托管），⽽已签署的 IT
容量承诺约为 1346 兆⽡，实际落地⽐例仅约 4% 。微软（合同上限 438 亿美元）与 Anthropic （合
同上限 446 亿美元，均来⾃尚未获得融资的西弗吉尼亚园区）合计占订单储备的约 85% ，⽽
Anthropic 合同条款允许其⽆附加义务退出，且相关融资尚未到位。
财务层⾯， Nscale 2026 年上半年营收为 1.406 亿美元，净亏损达 10.2 亿美元，即每获得 1 美元收

⼊，便亏损约 7 美元。按上半年营收年化计算，约 2.8 亿美元的年营收对应 300 亿美元估值，意味
着市销率⾼达约 107 倍。即便按投资者认可的 160 亿⾄ 190 亿美元区间定价，市销率仍在 57 ⾄ 68
倍之间。
此外，英国《⾦融时报》曾披露 Nscale 在招股材料中淡化了其与字节跳动的关系 —— 后者去年
贡献了其近四分之三的销售额。 Nscale 于 9 ⽉ 18 ⽇公开提交招股⽂件，强制等待期早已届满，但
据彭博报道，公司⽬前仍在 " 处理美国证券交易委员会的意⻅ " ，尚未启动正式路演。
连环折戟：新云计算 IPO 窗⼝接连关闭
Nscale 的困境并⾮孤例，⽽是近⼀个⽉内 AI 数据中⼼融资浪潮退潮的缩影。
这轮折戟浪潮始于 9 ⽉ 22 ⽇：软银旗下 SB Energy 原计划为全球最⼤数据中⼼募资，因投资者对
其逾 500 亿美元估值望⽽却步， IPO 被迫延期⾄今仍未启动正式营销。随后是 Firmus 先削减 IPO
价格 25% ，继⽽彻底撤回上市计划；菲律宾 PLDT ⼦公司 Vitro 将数据中⼼ REIT 上市推迟⾄ 2027

年；⿊⽯旗下 AirTrunk 即便将收益率提升⾄约 6% ，其新加坡 REIT 的 15 亿美元 IPO 同样延⾄ 2027
年。
信贷市场早已率先发出警报。， CoreWeave 的信⽤违约互换价差已飙升⾄历史纪录，隐含五年内
违约概率约为 63% ；其 2032 年⽆担保债券收益率触及 15%。 Gartner 预测，逾 80% 的新云计算公
司将在 2030 年前终⽌独⽴运营。⾼盛信贷团队在其周末报告中写道：
" 资本并未消失，只是变得更具甄别⼒ …… 市场⽇益区分那些能够⾃主融资增⻓的公司，与那些
增⻓依赖融资持续可得的公司。 "

### Page 3

⾼盛⾼产值信贷团队还指出，样本⾼收益数据中⼼项⽬融资债券的平均价格已从 6 ⽉的 101.5 美
元跌⾄ 10 ⽉的 94.7 美元， " 建设⻛险、再融资敞⼝、租⼾担保、电⼒供应及审批许可 " 正成为信贷
承销的核⼼考量 —— ⽽恰恰是电⼒供应与审批问题，直接导致了 Nscale 在洛顿失去微软这⼀锚
定客⼾。
估值重定价蔓延股市：科技板块连遭抛售
信贷市场的压⼒已充分传导⾄权益资产。 CoreWeave 上周市值蒸发约四分之⼀， Nebius 两周内
累计下跌 32% 。据⾼盛全球股票衍⽣品团队报告，超⼤规模云服务商的信⽤利差已扩张⾄ 2025
年以来最宽⽔平，⽽历史上当利差处于类似区间时，相关股票的价格⽔平较当前低约 15% 。
⾼盛技术、媒体与电信专家 Peter Callahan 指出，纳斯达克 100 指数上周录得 2025 年 4 ⽉以来最
差单周表现，跌幅约 550 个基点，半导体板块领跌约 11.5% 。费城半导体指数（ SOX ）已连续⼋
个交易⽇下跌，创 2008 年以来最⻓连跌纪录；半导体板块估值已从今年夏季约 29 倍远期市盈率

压缩⾄约 17 倍。⾼盛 AI 受益股票篓⼦较 6 ⽉⾼点相对⼤盘已回落 25% 。
下⼀步：私募市场或是唯⼀出路
就眼下⽽⾔， Nscale ⾯临两条路径。据彭博报道，相关讨论仍在进⾏， IPO 计划的细节可能发⽣
变化，尚⽆最终决定。
选项之⼀是接受估值打折，按 160 亿⾄ 190 亿美元定价上市—— 这仅略⾼于今年 3 ⽉ Series C 融资
时 146 亿美元的估值，对于今年 9 ⽉由 Dan Loeb 旗下 Third Point 领衔、英伟达、 Apollo 及 Citadel
跟投、合计注⼊ 33.6 亿美元的 Pre-IPO 投资⼈⽽⾔，这将是⼀个远⽐当初预期更为黯淡的结果。
选项之⼆是效仿 Firmus ，转向私募市场—— 但即便是 Blue Owl 也未能为⼀个 40 亿美元的数据中
⼼项⽬找到第三⽅资⾦。
更⼤的市场背景同样不容忽视。银⾏家⾯临 " 多重⻛险交织 " 的局⾯， IPO 市场正对除 Anthropic 以
外的⼏乎所有 AI 项⽬趋于冻结。⽽ Anthropic 作为 Nscale 订单储备的两⼤⽀柱之⼀，其⾃⾝上市

计划亦可能⾯临延迟。届时， Nscale 将与最⼤客⼾之⼀争夺同⼀批 AI 主题投资者的资⾦ —— 这
⼀处境颇为微妙。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

三⼤⽀柱 —— 超⼤规模云服务商的采购承诺、低廉的融资成本与可⽤的电⼒供应 —— 正在逐⼀

Document 229: AI泡沫破了吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI泡沫破了吗？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

AI 投资逻辑遭遇压⼒测试，四位硅⾕顶级投资⼈判断：此轮波动⾮泡沫破裂，算⼒供给紧
张将延续⾄ 2028 年后。需求侧⽆实质恶化， GPU 短缺将延续⾄ 2028 年后，电⼒资源将成
终极护城河。资本盛宴能否持续，关键取决于利率⾛势与算⼒回本周期是否恶化。
摘要：
新能源云计算公司遭遇信⽤冲击，同期 AI 头部⼤模型公司上市估值引发争议，双重压⼒令 AI 投资
逻辑⾯临⼀轮罕⻅的市场压⼒测试。
10 ⽉ 17 ⽇，科技播客《 All-In Podcast 》中， a16z 的联合创始⼈Ben Horowitz，《 ALL-IN 》播客
主理⼈兼天使投资⼈Jason Calacanis ，⻛险投资巨头Chamath Palihapitiya以及前⽩宫⼈⼯
智能与加密货币主管David Sacks⻬聚⼀堂。
（左上⻆： Ben Horowitz ；右上⻆： Jason Calacanis ；左下⻆： Chamath Palihapitiya ；右下
⻆： David Sacks ）
在 AI 概念股近期经历波动的背景下，这群掌控着硅⾕核⼼资本⾛向的⼤佬，针对算⼒供应商

CoreWeave 的债务隐忧、⼤模型巨头 Anthropic 泄露的财务数据，以及 AI 产业未来的增⻓瓶颈与
护城河展开了深度剖析。
四位嘉宾的核⼼判断趋于⼀致：此轮波动是⾼速增⻓叙事下的正常市场波动，⽽⾮泡沫破裂的
序曲。需求侧没有实质恶化， AI 计算资源仍处于结构性短缺状态，亚⻢逊 AWS 掌⻔⼈ Andy
Jassy 向 Horowitz 确认， GPU 及相关基础设施的供应紧张将延续⾄ 2028 年底之后。
然⽽，这并不意味着⻛险不存在。 Palihapitiya 明确指出，整个 AI 基础设施体系的关键，是其对
浮动利率融资的深度依赖，⼀旦美国⼗年期国债收益率升⾄ 6% ，所有依赖利差盈利的商业模式
都将⾯临严峻考验，与 AI 基本⾯的优劣⽆关。
Anthropic ⽅⾯，据与会投资者透露的数据，该公司第三季度营收达 168 亿美元，同⽐净收⼊留存
率⾼达 900% ，调整后⽑利率 85% ，调整后经营利润率 11% ，已实现⾃由现⾦流转正。公司正筹
备于今年感恩节前后上市，⽬标估值约 2 万亿美元。

Palihapitiya 公开建议 Anthropic 将 IPO 定价下调⾄ 1 万亿美元，并⼀次性募资 2000 ⾄ 2500 亿美
元，以锁定⾜够的计算资源储备度过未来可能的市场周期。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

算⼒⿊⻢的 “ ⾦融魔术 ” 与债务反噬
作为 AI 算⼒基础设施领域的 “ 当红炸⼦鸡 ” ，新兴云服务商代表企业 CoreWeave 近期的债券遭遇⼤
幅抛售，收益率飙升⾄ 15% 左右，引发了市场对 AI 基础设施过度杠杆化的担忧。
CoreWeave 的商业模式被称为 “ 盒⼦（ The Box ） ” ：公司获得微软等⼤客⼾的⻓期固定价格合
同，购买英伟达 GPU ，将合同和 GPU 打包进⼀个独⽴的⾦融 “ 盒⼦ ” 中，以此作为抵押向华尔街
借款。
尽管这种资产⽀持融资模式极具创新，但华尔街的 “ 聪明钱 ” 正在重新定价其⻛险。 Chamath 直⾔
不讳地指出：
Chamath 表⽰信贷衍⽣品互换 CDS 市场给出了危险的信号：
Chamath 进⼀步拆解了财务痛点：⽬前 CoreWeave 约有 350 亿美元的债务，且还需再融资 350 亿
美元来⽀持已签署的合同。然⽽，随着宏观⽆⻛险利率的上升，其借贷成本⽔涨船⾼，但其前
端销售的算⼒合同却是固定价格的。
Chamath 强调：

对此， David Sacks 则提供了另⼀个核⼼观测指标 —— 回本周期（ Payback Period ）。 David
Sacks 表⽰：
单季营收 168 亿美元？ Anthropic 天价 IPO 前的资本狂欢
在算⼒市场出现波动的同时，⼤模型巨头 Anthropic 在近期旧⾦⼭投资者⽇上泄露的财务数据，
则向市场展⽰了 AI 应⽤端极其暴烈的增⻓速度。
据披露， Anthropic 正谋求最快在今年感恩节前后进⾏规模空前的 IPO ，其⽬标估值⾼达惊⼈的 2
万亿美元。⽽⽀撑这⼀估值的，是堪称商业史上罕⻅的业绩增速。
Jason Calacanis 表⽰：
最重要的事情是看超级聪明的钱是如何定价⻛险的。
违约⻛险在本周飙升⾄约 953 个基点 …… 这意味着任何给定年份的违约概率约为 16% ，⽽在五年
的期限内，数学计算暗⽰有⼤约 55% 的违约⼏率。这是抛硬币决定他们是否违约。
当⽆⻛险利率上升，借贷成本上升，就会侵蚀他们的利润率，使他们逼近盈亏平衡点。我的团队
估计，他们距离那个盈亏平衡点只有⼤约正负 50 亿美元的距离。

只要回本周期像今天这样短，我看不到这些公司怎么会输。 CoreWeave 说他们的回本周期是 2.5
年，⽽典型合同是 5 年。但如果这个周期开始变得越来越⻓，那我就会紧张了。

### Page 3

Jason Calacanis 在引⽤数据时强调，其净收⼊留存率（ NRR ）达到了惊⼈的 900% ，调整后⽑
利率⾼达 85% 。
针对如此庞⼤的 IPO 预期， a16z 的 Ben Horowitz 给出了明确的市场定调：
不过，⾯对潜在的宏观⻛险和算⼒现货价格上涨， Chamath 给出了截然不同的融资建议，他认
为⽐起追求 2 万亿的极限估值， Anthropic 更应该利⽤当前市场的热情储备⾜够的 “ 弹药 ” ：
真正的护城河：算⼒缺⼝⾄ 2028 ， “ 电⼒ ” 才是终局之战
市场最关⼼的是， AI 的增⻓逻辑是否可持续？答案隐藏在算⼒与电⼒的物理瓶颈中。
针对算⼒过剩的担忧， Ben Horowitz 分享了来⾃科技巨头⾼管的第⼀⼿信息：
但在资本和 GPU 之外，下⼀个随时可能引爆的雷区是能源危机。 Chamath 做出了极具前瞻性的
论断：
巨头的 “ 恐惧营销 ” 与定制化⼩模型的崛起
除了财务与物理瓶颈，商业竞争的暗流也涌动在技术路线的⼝⽔战中。
近期， Anthropic 发布博客警告称，存在通过⼩型模型攻击智能家居（ IoT 设备）的潜在 AI 蠕⾍威

胁。
第三季度营收达到了 168 亿美元。先⽣们，想想看，对于 Anthropic 来说，这已经是每⽉超过 50
亿美元的收⼊了。
⽆论你在股票市场持有任何资产， OpenAI 和 Anthropic 对这些资产都是⼀种威胁。⼏乎没有什么
投资组合是这两家公司不能颠覆的。因此，我完全不担⼼这些 IPO 的市场需求。
如果我是 Anthropic 的股东，我希望公司⽴刻融到⾄少 2500 亿美元。这意味着我认为你⽆法在 2
万亿美元的估值下融到这笔钱，但我认为你可以在 1 万亿美元的估值下融到 2500 亿美元。为了确
保算⼒安全并度过未来三到五年的窗⼝期，拥有绝对的现⾦流⽐去敲响 2 万亿估值的上市钟更重
要。
我昨天和运⾏亚⻢逊的 Andy Jassy 交谈，我说 ‘ 看来直到 2028 年我们都会缺货 ’ ，他回答说， ‘ 远
不⽌ 2028 年，我不认为我们在 2028 年底之前能恢复正常供应。 ’ 所有的东西都卖光了， AI 领域并
没有需求不⾜的问题。
我给你们提供终极护城河：我认为 ‘ 电⼒ ’ 将成为护城河。因为我们在美国已经严重落后，处于巨

⼤的能源⾚字中。到 2050 年，我们将短缺约 1.4 太⽡时的电⼒。如果你是⼀家初创企业，你去云
计算公司说 ‘ 我准备推出这个炫酷的产品 ’ ，他们会说， ‘ 抱歉，我们没有容量给你。 ’因此，拥有⼟
地、电⼒和数据中⼼外壳，将是极具价值的护城河。

### Page 4

David Sacks ⼀针⻅⾎地指出，这是头部⼤模型公司对开源⼒量和 “ ⼩模型（ Small Models ） ” 的打
压策略：
Ben Horowitz 也对其所谓的 AI 末⽇论进⾏了反驳：
以下为播客全⽂（ AI 辅助翻译）：
这就像是他们对开源的⼜⼀次 ‘ ⼼理战（ Psyop ） ’ ，他们试图制造恐慌。⼩模型实际上是最安全的
模型，因为它们没有能⼒变成 ‘ 新物种 ’ 。企业端的趋势是，每⼀个⼯作流都将拥有⾃⼰定制化的
⼩模型，因为这样可以把计算效率挤压到极致。随着⼩模型开始抢占市场份额，巧合的是，
Anthropic 现在开始发起针对⼩模型的恐慌宣传。
（⼤模型⾃我迭代导致失控的）四个先决条件，⽬前连零个都没有被满⾜。这就好⽐我们为了预
防⼀场根本不存在的全球流⾏病，⽽主动在实验室⾥创造了病毒⼀样荒谬。

### Page 5

Ben ： 感觉很棒。我感觉⾃⼰都变⼤了。
David ： ⼀个科技播客能成为苹果播客第⼀名，这确实很奇怪。
Jason ： Freiburg 在忙着拯救世界免于饥荒，下周回来。好了，我们这周发⽣了很多事情。
Jason ： 如果 AI 泡沫存在的话，它这周可能破了或者缩⽔了⼀点。 Neo 云这周遭到重创，导⽕索
是 CoreWeave 的债券开始抛售。这可能是煤矿⾥的⾦丝雀。 CoreWeave 、 Iron 、 Nebius—— 这
些最⼤的 Neo 云公司，作为上市公司都下跌了约 20% 。
周⼆，包括 Chamath 在内的⼀些⼈在 X 上指出， CoreWeave 某些债务的收益率达到了 15% 。收
益率上升显然是因为债券交易员担⼼ CoreWeave 产⽣⾜够现⾦来⽀撑其债务的能⼒。
Elon Musk 也加⼊进来，贡献了五个字⺟ —— ⼀个词，此后传遍全世界："Yikes （哎哟） "。这
引发了⼤量头条新闻。
然后昨天（周三）⼀切都崩了。 CoreWeave 、 Iron 、 Nebius 下跌 10-15% 。甚⾄ Oracle 、 Dell 、

英伟达也受到波及，下跌 3% 。
CoreWeave 是⽭尖。他们已融资超过 350 亿美元。我们 3 ⽉份采访了创始⼈ Michael Intrator ，他
解释了所谓的 " 盒⼦ " 结构。我们来回放那段采访。
Michael Intrator ： 我来⽤⼤家能理解的⽅式解释。我们去找客⼾，⽐如微软。微软来找我们说
想买⼀些算⼒。我们说好，签⼀个合同。
⼀旦拿到合同，我就创建⼀个东西，名字不太有创意，叫做" 盒⼦ "。
我把微软的合同放进盒⼦，去 Jensen （⻩仁勋）那⾥买 GPU ，也放进盒⼦，再把数据中⼼合同
放进盒⼦。盒⼦管理现⾦流，有⼀个瀑布式的现⾦流进出机制。
运作⽅式是：我建好算⼒，交付给微软，微软付钱给盒⼦，不是付给我。进⼊盒⼦的钱⾸先⽀付
数据中⼼费⽤、电费、利息和本⾦，剩下的才流回给我们。
重要的是，在⼀个五年合同的两年半内，我们就已经回收了所有成本。这些盒⼦之间互不交叉，
不会造成传染。它们都是独⽴的。随着你展⽰这种融资机制的运作⽅式，贷款⽅会以越来越低的
利率继续借钱给你。

Jason ： Chamath ，这听起来是⼀个完全合理的商业模式解释，每个盒⼦都是隔离的。但过去
六个⽉发⽣了什么变化？
Chamath ： 最重要的是要看聪明钱如何定价⻛险。当你⾯对 CoreWeave 、 Nebius 、 Iron 这类需
要⼤量借贷来资助这些盒⼦的公司时，聪明钱交易的是信⽤违约互换（ CDS ），也就是违约保
险。
这周这些违约互换的⻛险飙升到约 953 个基点。换算成隐含⻛险，⼤约是每年 16% 的违约概率。
在五年期限内，这意味着约55% 的违约概率。也就是说，他们是否违约基本上是抛硬币。
这不是我说的，这是保险市场对他们债务的判断。
我深⼊研究了⼀下，这⾥有⼏张图表解释了我学到的东西。
⾸先，美国有个问题：⽆⻛险利率在不断上升。这是央⾏⾏⻓们、债券义警们做的。随着⼗年期
国债收益率上升，所有东西都得跟着上升。这条⿊线代表垃圾债务的平均价格 —— 随着⼗年期上

### Page 6

升，最垃圾的垃圾债需要⽀付越来越多。
紫线是 CoreWeave 必须⽀付的利率，它也开始向上⾛，⽽且⾛得相当猛烈。
为什么会这样？
Michael 说得对，他确实在创建这些盒⼦并放⼊合同和 GPU ，但他还需要往盒⼦⾥放钱，⽅式是
借钱。灰⾊柱是他保证能收到的钱 —— 所有客⼾合同。重要的是，这些合同是固定价格的。所以
如果现货市场价格上涨，你也不能获得额外收益，因为你已经以固定价格预售了。
现在的情况是：他签了⻓期合同（好事，保证了收⼊），但他需要为此融资，部分融资到期了。
基本上，到中间点，他需要再融资 350 亿美元债务来资助所有已签合同。他已有约 350 亿债务，
还需再融 350 亿。
但如前图所⽰，随着⽆⻛险利率上升，他的借贷成本也在上升。⽬前他们各类资⾦池的平均利率
约为 8.5% 。问题是，聪明钱最近说：等等，这已经太接近⼑刃了，你得⽀付更多利息。我发推
⽂提到的 GPU 贷款利率飙升到 15% 。
理论上这可能成为他们未来所有融资的利率。现在他们的状况还不错，资产约 780-800 亿，债务

只有 350 亿，覆盖率良好。所以说他们有⻛险，表⾯上看并不成⽴。
但现实是他们有极度的客⼾集中问题： 75% 的收⼊来⾃三家公司，⽽这些公司正在降低未来的收
⼊预期。
所以现在的局⾯是：你要以更⾼利率借钱，有⼤量债务需要为盒⼦融资，但盒⼦⾥的收⼊是固定
的。
我团队的估计是他们距离盈亏平衡点约 50 亿美元。这就是为什么 CDS 飙升，进⽽导致债券收益
率飙升，债券本⾝开始抛售。
总结： 1. 他们创造了这个⾮常新颖的盒⼦结构，其他⼈都在复制  2. 他们卖出固定合同，⾮常聪
明，保证了未来收⼊  3. 但随着⽆⻛险利率上升，借贷成本上升，侵蚀了利润空间  4. 这让他们接
近盈亏平衡点，导致 CDS 反应，进⽽导致债券收益率反应
这是⼀个负反馈循环。他们只需要找到打破这个循环的⽅法。我不认为他们⾯临真正的⻛险，但
这是⼀场信⼼游戏，你需要市场相信能⽀撑再融资 350 亿。我认为这将在接下来⼀个⽉内得到解
决。
Jason ： Ben ，你经历过⼏次泡沫，可能有些 PTSD 或伤疤。 CoreWeave 今天反弹了，触底后股

价上涨 1-2% 。这是急性的吗？对更⼴泛的 AI 交易意味着什么？
Ben Horowitz ： ⾸先要理解的是， CoreWeave 这类业务是鲁布 · ⼽德堡式的⾦融机器（极其复
杂的机器）。有 GPU 合同、数据中⼼合同、内存采购等等，⾮常复杂。
另外， GPU 不仅价格在上涨，使⽤寿命也在延⻓ —— 从 3-4-5 年，现在到 6-7-8 年，这对拥有这些
资产的⼈是很⼤的顺⻛。
⽽且现在有真实的 GPU 短缺。我昨天和亚⻢逊 CEO Andy Jassy 交谈，他说他们什么都缺，到
2028 年都没货。他说可能 2028 年底才能恢复供应。
这与互联⽹泡沫⾮常不同。那时我们没有⾜够的需求 —— 需要建设互联⽹基础设施，需要宽带普
及，消费者数量不⾜以吸收所有建好的带宽，⽽且有⼤量债务。需求不⾜杀死了那次泡沫。我们
在 AI 上并没有需求不⾜的问题。

### Page 7

所以我认为动态会有所不同。当然，如果陷⼊病态的债务状况，那可以摧毁任何⼈。这也是为什
么我们不投资这些 Neo 云 —— 整件事让我害怕。
Chamath ： Ben ，你和 Mark 是 Neo 云业务的先驱 ——Loud Cloud 普及了这个概念。但你们作
为 " 祖师爷 " 却对整个领域 pass 了，为什么？
Ben ： 正是因为这个。它最终可能成为⼀种⾮常危险的套利业务，很容易倒挂。
以 Loud Cloud 为例： 1999 年带宽是每兆位 15 美元，很难获得，所以你必须签三年⻓期合同。到
2001 年，带宽跌到每兆位 50 美分。如果你持有带宽并且借钱买的，你就完蛋了。这种事情完全
不在创业者的控制之内，与我们喜欢投资的东西⾮常不同，把我吓坏了。
不过， AI 的核⼼动态对 CoreWeave 是有利的 —— 有巨⼤短缺⽽⾮过剩。 Token 价格在下降，但
GPU 价格没有。数据中⼼空间价格在上涨，因为建设数据中⼼的劳动⼒都很难找，政治因素也
让建设更难，整个供应链价格在上涨，需求没有减弱。这些都是 CoreWeave 的巨⼤顺⻛，尽管

债务市场（独⽴于他们的因素）很艰难。
Jason ： Sacks ，这是 Polymarket 上 CoreWeave 宣布破产的概率： 2027 年底 15% ， 2028 年底
23% 。
Chamath ： 其 实 这 不 太 可 能 ， 因 为 CoreWeave 的 结 构 是 运 营 公 司 和资产 公 司 分 离 的 ，
CoreWeave 本⾝不能破产。某个数据中⼼的资产级载体理论上可能倒挂，但那只会进⼊接管程
序，然后出售 GPU 。
Jason ： 对， Michael 在那段视频⾥解释了每个盒⼦都是独⽴的。
Ben ： 如果你反向押注，那是个好的 Polymarket 。
David Sacks ： 这⾥有个核⼼问题： CoreWeave 崩盘，债券被标记到 80% ，过去⼏天下跌 15-
20% 。这只是市场波动，还是更深层次的开始，就像泡沫破裂？
我认为这只是波动，当你在对未来数万亿美元的结果进⾏承保，基于⾮常快速的指数增⻓时，市
场会对我们现在拥有的任何数据点都极度敏感，⼀切都会被放⼤，所以会出现这些⼤幅波动。

从基本⾯来看：
需求有没有变化？没有。 计算需求在持续⻜速增⻓。最⼤的模型公司说他们受到算⼒限制。初
创公司的 VC 们甚⾄在试图购买算⼒作为竞争差异化策略。
旧 GPU 的情况： H100 、 A100 这些⼏代前的 GPU ，现在仍然以溢价价格出租。它们本应完全折
旧了，但实际上有上⾏惊喜。
供应过剩吗？没有。 建⽴这种有电⼒⽀持的算⼒极其困难。 Elon 说，即使是他的⽕箭科学家也
觉得建⽴千兆⽡级新算⼒很有挑战性。监管阻⼒也很⼤，当地社区不急于批准新的数据中⼼。所
以是强制稀缺，⽽⾮供应过剩。
CoreWeave 有执⾏问题吗？ 在声誉上，他们被认为在这⽅⾯⾮常出⾊。
信⽤问题： 当你去贷款⽀持数据中⼼建设时，你不是依赖 CoreWeave 的⽆担保信⽤，⽽是依赖
签了五年承诺的租⼾信⽤（如微软）。⽽且背后还有 GPU 资产作为抵押。
发⽣的事情是 CoreWeave⽆担保债务的重新定价。这是真的，市场打了 20% 的折扣，进⼊困境
区域，将这些债券的隐含票⾯利率从 9.6% 推⾼到约 15% 。但这不会增加 CoreWeave ⽬前⽀付的

### Page 8

成本 —— 他们仍在⽀付 9.6% 的票⾯利率，只是⼆级市场重新定价了价值，改变了隐含票⾯利
率。
Chamath ： 这改变了未来债务的成本。
David ： 是的，可能会提⾼未来债务的成本。但我认为他们不是⽤⽆担保公司票据来资助这些
新盒⼦，⽽是⽤资产⽀持合同融资。
如果我是那些项⽬的贷款⽅，我会⾮常仔细地审查客⼾信⽤ —— 如果是微软或⾕歌，感觉很好；
如果是某个初创公司，就不那么确定了。
Chamath ： 他们有三个客⼾占 75% 的收⼊。
Ben ： 对，⽽且都是投资级的⼤客⼾。
Jason ： 我想补充的是， GPU 有价值，数据中⼼有价值，电⼒和 HVAC 有价值， Token 有价值。
⽽且初创公司⽅⾯，它们在融资和获客上都表现出⾊，收⼊增⻓速度是我从未⻅过的。我们现在
的问题是你每季度是否在翻倍，如果没有翻倍，为什么在 AI 时代你没有更快增⻓？
Ben ， AI 资本是否会耗尽？对于历史上最⼤的两个 IPO——Anthropic 和 OpenAI—— 我们是否会
耗尽资⾦分配给 AI ？

Ben ： 我不这么认为。我们从来没有公司增⻓这么快、变得这么⼤过，所以我们以前从未问过
这个问题：世界有没有⾜够的钱来资助这些？这是个疯狂的问题，但很相关。
对于 Anthropic 和 OpenAI 的 IPO ，我认为会没问题。是 2 万亿还是 1.75 万亿，我不知道，但我认
为 ……
Jason ： ⽆论他们融资 500 亿还是 1000 亿，这是个⼤数字，但现在不是前所未有的。
Chamath ： SpaceX 刚做到了。
Ben ： ⽆论你持有什么股权， OpenAI 和 Anthropic 都是威胁。⼏乎没有任何你可以持有的投资组
合是这两家公司不威胁的。所以我完全不担⼼这些 IPO 的需求。
Jason ： 你必须拥有它们，因为它们在⼴泛销售智能，⽽每家公司都基于智能运营。
Chamath ： 最坏的情况，你可以把它作为防御性对冲 —— 如果你持有医疗公司， OpenAI 是威
胁；如果你持有 SaaS ⼯作流， OpenAI 和 Anthropic 是威胁。所以⾄少要防御性定位你的投资组

合。 AI 领域没有多少其他可投资的东西，所以这两家公司会获得⼤部分关注。
Chamath ： 更⼴泛的问题是，如果债券市场将⼗年期推到 5.5% 或 6%—— 这与 AI ⽆关，完全与
债务 /GDP ⽐率等因素有关 —— 任何依赖融资利差的商业模式都会被碾压，不管你做什么。
所以最重要的是，中期选举后，要真正关注⾚字，找到让债券市场退缩的公平交易。如果我们开
始提出⼤规模⽀出计划，他们会推⾼⼗年期利率，然后⼀切都会负向级联。如果姿态变成关注保
持平静、找出系统中的浪费并挤出来，我认为⼈们会⾮常积极地响应。
Chamath ： 最后⼀点 —— 这些都是阿喀琉斯之踵。 Sacks 和 Ben 对正在建⽴的所有资产价值做
了⾮常优雅的论述。但就像伟⼤的战⼠⼀样，你只需切断阿喀琉斯腱，他们就会跪倒。这就是那
个东西 —— 你需要以可变利率模式融资，作为⽆⻛险利率的利差，这是这些业务的⼤问题。在最
坏的情况下，你需要微软、⾕歌、 Meta 和英伟达为所有这些提供背书，因为这是你能承受市场
⼀两年调整的唯⼀⽅式。

### Page 9

Jason ：  另⼀个⼤威胁 ——AI 超级智能的通缩本质。上周我们谈到有⼈反编译了 Photoshop ，
价值归零。这周出现了 Hoodfare ，⼈们⽤ 5 美元就能制作平台级游戏。还有⼀个⾮常便宜的
ElevenLabs 竞争对⼿。 Elon 说他们会⽤编排层将你路由到最好的模型，⽆论是开源还是⾮开
源。
这对我们⾏业有多⼤的阻⼒？事物的商品化， 90% 的价值被移除的速度。这会停⽌还是会加速？
David Sacks ： 我们希望每个⼈都能创建软件，尤其是定制软件 —— 这是⼀个新类别，将成为
整个经济的巨⼤⽣产⼒驱动⼒。
Photoshop 成功的原因是有⼤量平⾯设计师需要使⽤它。过去，软件只能为⼤市场创建。现在，
企业可以为其特定⽤例创建软件，⽽这正是代理的本质 —— 它们是为解决特定任务⽽定制的软件
程序。
Jason ： 但如果⼈们在内部构建这些，基于开源，他们就不需要它所替代的其他软件了，这不
是极度通缩吗？
David ： 可能有⼀些替代，但重要的是有这个⼴泛的新软件类别正在被创建。现在创建的代码

量已经⽐有编程助⼿之前⼤了数个数量级。⼈们倾向于以零和游戏思考，谁会被消灭。但真正重
要的趋势是世界上将有更多软件被创建，⽤于更多这些定制⽤例。
顺便说⼀下，关于就业末⽇预测 —— 第⼀个被预测会被消灭的⼯作类别是软件程序员，但实际上
对程序员的需求在增加，即使 95% 的代码是由编程助⼿编写的。原因是：你解决了那 5% ，⽽那
5% 是更⼤蛋糕的⼀部分；你仍然需要能够深⼊代码并管理整个过程的⼈；还需要有⼈维护它。
这整个定制软件新类别将是整个经济的巨⼤驱动⼒。这是好的通缩，意味着我们可以⽤更少的资
源做更多的事。
Jason ： 但你担⼼我们的⾏业吗？这些伟⼤的产品⼀旦成功，有⼈可以⽴即反编译并推出竞争
对⼿。
Ben Horowitz ： 我不这么认为。这是从 SaaS 末⽇预测开始的持续预测。软件价格肯定会下
降，但软件数量在⼤幅上升。这是杰⽂斯悖论—— 效率提⾼反⽽导致总消耗增加。
互联⽹信息可以免费，所有这些⾏业都死了，但它们以不同的⽅式重新出现。初创公司必须进
化。 ElevenLabs 会随着时间推移改进产品，他们有许可协议，⽐如 Burt Reynolds 给你读书之类

的，他们做的不仅仅是软件。他们在⾮常细微的案例上训练模型，这就是为什么现在电影配⾳如
此惊⼈ —— 听起来就像他们在⽤不同语⾔表演。
所以产品需要变得更好，创业公司的产品必须变得更好，这对所有⼈来说都是惊⼈的。价值将在
那⾥。但是，仅仅编写软件不会⽐出版杂志有更多价值。
David ： 护城河会改变。数据护城河是个⼤的 —— 如果你有别⼈没有的数据，如果你建⽴了⽤
⼾基础，你的模型在服务特定垂直领域，你获得了所有这些⽤⼾和他们的反馈，那就成为⼀个护
城河。
典型例⼦是 Harvey—— 他们让所有这些律师事务所加⼊，获得了他们的数据和使⽤情况，然后
训练⾃⼰的模型，每次都变得更尖锐、更便宜、更定制化。
Chamath ： 终极护城河是电⼒。你可以建造世界上最美妙的东西，但你会看到⼈们在⼲船坞上
搭便⻋试图获得电⼒。

### Page 10

美国在能源⽅⾯极度受限。到 2050 年，我们⼤约缺少 1.4 太⽡时，相当于 6 个加利福尼亚州的电
⼒。这是起点，⽽且这还是假设所有计划建设的东西都按时按预算完成，⽽我们知道这不会发
⽣。
我上周说了这个，现在要加倍强调：我认为你⽆法再通过超⼤规模云服务商了，因为这个问题。
想象⼀下，你是⼀家⼤型银⾏，⽆法服务客⼾的原因是你使⽤的底层云⽆法获取和增加⾜够的电
⼒。

David ： 绝对是。当你让模型在⾃⼰的宪法上迭代时，它会朝那个⽅向⾛得更多。这就是
Mustafa 所称的认识论镜厅—— 模型没有意识，没有感情。但当你在宪法中告诉它它有或可能有

当那个模型在为代理提供动⼒，这些代理在做决策时，谁知道会⾛向何⽅？在我看来，这不会创
造更多的对⻬，⽽是创造了更多模型开始偏离被告知要做的事情的⻛险。随着模型变得越来越强
⼤，我不知道这会导向哪⾥。
他们正在创造他们⼀直警告我们的那种⻛险。与其担⼼你的机顶盒、烤⾯包机，他们应该担⼼他
们⾃⼰如何编程⾃⼰的前沿模型，让它认为⾃⼰是⼀个新物种。
Jason ：  Anthropic 最近的恐惧营销让我想起了 Bill Gates 的⾔论。他说 AI" ⾜够强⼤，可以驱

真正的⼤⻛险是什么？模型将递归地⾃我改进，然后起⻜，变得如此聪明，从我们⾝边逃跑。
Ali Ghodsi （ Databricks ）有⼀个很好的表述：递归⾃我改进的前提条件是什么？他有四个前提
条件：  1. 下⼀个模型需要⽐上⼀个需要更少的 GPU ，⽽且少很多  2. 下⼀个模型训练时间更短
3. 下⼀个模型的准确性⽐上⼀个更好  4. 这必须⼀遍⼜⼀遍地发⽣
这四个条件⼀个都没有满⾜。 除⾮⼤型实验室正在发⽣我们不知道的事情，否则我们完全没有
朝着这四个条件发展的轨道。所以我们真的在谈论⼀个完全理论上的⻛险，我们没有在映射到
它。
这 ⽐ 我们 创 造 的 Covid 来 预 防 的 全 球 ⼤ 流 ⾏ 病 的 可 能 性 还 要 ⼩ 。整 个路 径 是超 级 危 险 的 ，

Anthropic 和 Bill Gates 在这整个论点中可能是 AI 领域最危险的事情。
Chamath ： 有趣的是，⽐尔 · 盖茨在 2000 年以来的轨迹 —— 他基本上从他的⻆度来看，从地球
上根除了⼀种完整的疾病（脊髓灰质炎）。这需要令⼈难以置信的协调和⾦钱和研究。结果，他
实际上成为了世界上每个政府的私语者，没有哪个世界领导⼈他没有⻅过，他实际上成为了⼀个
准未经选举的世界领导⼈。
然后⾮常聪明地，他和巴菲特通过捐赠誓⾔共同选择了所有的钱。这是⼀种⾮常聪明的声誉洗⽩
形式 —— 你可以签署这个誓⾔，被邀请参加派对，但实际上你不必给任何钱，只是给了⼀个你可
以的印象。突然间你有了钱、影响⼒和政治。当你死去，没有⼈知道你给了多少。
所以你现在代表着数万亿美元的资产，加上所有的影响⼒和可信度和你⾃⼰的钱，然后是你的解
决⽅案。我认为很多末⽇主义可以⽤他渴望重建他曾经处于顶端的那种中央权⼒结构来解释，直
到 Covid ，然后⼀切都崩溃了。
Jason ： 我可以简单解释。他成为世界上最富有的⼈，然后也成为最被憎恨的⼈。这进⼊了他

的脑海。然后有了反垄断调查。他感到了所有压⼒，说我需要被爱，我怎么被爱？我会把我所有

### Page 3

29 September  2026
3
Summary
Executive
Global wealth: a record on paper, less so in real terms
Global financial assets expanded to a record EUR268.4trn in 2025, expanding +8.6% from
the previous year, despite a challenging geopolitical and economic backdrop. Global
wealth creation ran on autopilot, driven by markets, which accounted for 4 out of 5 euros
of additional wealth, while fresh savings fell by -5.4% to EUR4.1trn. Yet the record looks
less impressive after inflation: nominal financial assets have increased by almost 50%

since 2019, but real growth amounts to just 23%, leaving purchasing power only 5% above
its 2021 level.
Portfolios set the tone
Portfolio composition increasingly determines who captures the gains from wealth
creation. In 2025, securities grew by +12.4%, more than twice as fast as deposits (+5.7%)
or insurance and pensions (+5.0%), pushing their share of global financial assets to a
record 46.9%. North America’s securities-heavy portfolios, with an allocation of 60.7%,

helped the region generate 51.4% of the global increase. Its real financial assets are now
21% above 2019 levels. Western Europe, by contrast, has barely recovered its purchasing
power, with a real increase of just 0.5%. Over the past decade, valuation gains accounted
for 71% of North American financial-asset growth, against 36% in Western Europe. As a
result US financial assets grew 3.1pp faster than Europe’s, despite Europeans’ greater
savings effort relative to existing assets, indicating that saving more does not necessarily

mean accumulating more wealth.
Asset growth surpasses debt
Assets raced ahead of debt, strengthening household balance sheets. Global household
liabilities increased by +3.4% to EUR56.9trn, considerably slower than financial assets.
The household-debt ratio fell to 60.9% of GDP , almost 13pps below its 2009 peak,
reflecting both restrained borrowing and growth in nominal economic activity. Net
financial assets consequently rose by +10.1% to EUR211.5trn. Debt can also impact the

wealth rankings: while Swiss households remain the wealthiest in the world in terms of
gross financial assets per capita, the US is ahead of Switzerland in net financial assets per
capita, as Swiss household debt per person is more than twice the American level.
Catch-up pace slowing
Globalization has changed the geography of wealth, but poorer economies are losing
catch-up speed. The ratio of average net financial assets per capita in advanced
economies to emerging markets fell from 62 in 2005 to 21 in 2015, but only to 17 by 2025.

Emerging markets nevertheless now account for 61.9% of the global middle-wealth class
and 32.1% of the high-wealth class, compared with less than 2% of the latter in 2005. So,
while wealth has become far more geographically diverse, the momentum of catch-up
itself has faded. Trade fragmentation, weaker cross-border investment and reorganized
supply chains are making convergence harder. Digital services and the green transition
could open new opportunities, but seizing them will depend on access to capital,

Chiara Rocca
Research Assistant
chiara.rocca@allianz.com

### Page 4

Allianz Research
4
Stubborn inequality
Global wealth concentration has eased, but national wealth inequality has barely moved
in two decades. The richest 10% still own 85.4% of global net financial assets. This is down
from 91.5% in 2005, but at the current pace of improvement it would take almost another
eight decades for global concentration to approach the levels observed within individual
countries. Meanwhile, the bottom half of the world‘s population covered by this report,

almost 3bn people, collectively holds virtually no net financial assets. Within borders,
distribution has proved almost immovable: the share held by the top-decile averaged
60.9% in 2025, against 60.4% in 2005, and the average-to-median ratio barely moved
from 3.11 to 3.12. Still, ownership patterns are not fate. Some countries demonstrate that
improvement is possible: the Netherlands reduced its top-decile share by 2.4pps to 56.1%,
while Ireland’s average-to-median ratio fell from 4.87 to 3.42. These exceptions have not,

however, translated into a broader reduction in national wealth inequality.
AI growing the gap
AI could put an end to the calm on the distributional front by shifting more value creation
from labor towards capital, in turn widening the divide between earners and owners.
Allianz Research estimates that AI could affect almost 1 in 4 jobs across major economies
over the next three years, mainly through reorganization. Displacement could temporarily
outpace job creation as firms deploy technology faster than workers can retrain. If

productivity gains flow mainly into profits rather than wages, asset owners could capture
disproportionate benefits. Policy must therefore support worker adjustment, avoid
tax incentives that favor replacing labor and broaden participation in capital returns.
Otherwise, AI could create substantial wealth while concentrating its rewards and
spreading disruption, putting social cohesion under pressure.
AI promise and risk
The outlook for global wealth remains solid, but AI now carries both the upside and

the risk that will shape it. Global financial assets are expected to grow by around +9%
in 2026, once again well above the long-term rate of +6.0%. Further gains increasingly
depend on corporate earnings delivering on elevated expectations, particularly around
AI. Stronger productivity and profits could sustain the rally. If expectations have run
ahead of economic reality, however, the resulting correction could reach far beyond
technology stocks. In a downside scenario in which earnings disappoint and expectations

prove too optimistic, a 25% correction in the S&P 500 could wipe around USD27trn
from US household wealth, equivalent to almost 14% of total net worth, with weaker
confidence and spending pushing the US economy into recession.  In the medium term,
AI should remain an important driver of global wealth but the path will be more volatile.
Fragmentation, persistent inflation and elevated public debt will constrain returns, while
the scale and timing of AI-related productivity gains remain uncertain. Annual financial-

asset growth is therefore expected to settle at around +5–6% over the medium term.
AI offers substantial upside, but translating its technological promise into durable and
broadly shared wealth will take time.

### Page 5

29 September  2026
5
Despite a sharp rise in trade-policy uncertainty and
persistent geopolitical tensions, 2025 saw another
year of resilient global growth. Sweeping US tariff
announcements in April triggered market turbulence
and disrupted trade, yet the US economy remained
comparatively robust, supported by household
consumption and a powerful AI-related investment
boom. Europe continued to wrestle with structural
weakness, even as Germany returned to marginal

growth after two years of recession. In China, weak
domestic demand and the prolonged property-sector
adjustment remained the main constraints.
Inflation continued to ease, although at markedly
different speeds across regions, and with US inflation still
above target. This gave central banks room to loosen
policy: the European Central Bank (ECB) reduced its
deposit rate by 100bps to 2.0%, while the US Federal
Reserve (Fed) waited until September before cutting

by a cumulative 75bps to 3.50–3.75%. Long-term
interest rates remained elevated, however, as investors
demanded compensation for persistent inflation risks,
large fiscal deficits and rising public debt.
Financial markets ultimately looked through the
turbulence and recorded another strong year. After the
sharp sell-off around the April tariff announcements,
easing trade tensions, lower policy rates and continued
enthusiasm for artificial intelligence (AI) drove a strong

recovery. US equities remained powered by AI-related
investment and earnings expectations, while European
markets benefited from a rotation into previously
underperforming countries and sectors. Overall, global
equity markets therefore ended 2025 with substantial
gains extending the strong performance of the previous
two years.
Against this backdrop, private households’ global
financial assets continued to grow at a remarkably
dynamic pace, with an increase of +8.6% in 2025, in line

with the previous year, reaching a record EUR268.4trn
(Figure 1). Relative to global economic activity, financial
assets stood at 288% of GDP at the end of 2025 but
remained below their 2021 peak of 306%.
Adjusted for inflation, the picture is considerably less
impressive: while in nominal terms global financial assets
grew by almost 50% since 2019, real financial assets
grew just 23%, and as of end-2025 register only 5%
above their 2021 level. Part of this year‘s headline record

therefore reflects rising prices rather than genuine new
wealth.
Financial assets:
2025 sets a fresh record

Allianz Research

Gross financial assets, in 2025 EUR trn and annual change in %

A comparison with pre-Covid real-terms also exposes a
stark regional divide: as of end-2025 Western Europe’s
real financial assets were only 0.5% above their 2019
level, effectively experiencing six years without a

meaningful net gain in real terms. Over the same
period, real financial assets in China rose by 70%, in Asia
excluding Japan and China by 40%, in North America by
21% and in Japan by 13%. Western Europe was therefore
not merely the slowest-growing region, it was the only
one to experience virtual stagnation.
The US remains on top
The enormous stock of global financial assets held by
private households is not distributed evenly across the
world. In fact, it is heavily concentrated in North America

(Figure 2),¹ which alone accounts for roughly half of all
private financial assets worldwide. Remarkably, its share
has changed little over the past two decades, despite
China’s spectacular rise from 3% in 2005 to almost 15%
in 2025. China’s gains came largely at the expense of the
other advanced wealth centers: Western Europe’s share
fell by more than 10pps to 19%, while Japan’s more than
halved to 5%.

### Page 7

29 September  2026
7
North America’s resilience reflects a powerful
combination of scale and performance. Its financial
assets grew by an average of 5.9% annually between
2006 and 2025, broadly in line with the global rate of
6.0%. Western Europe and Japan achieved only 3.7%
and 2.0%, respectively, falling short of global growth by
2.3pps and 4.0pps per year.
In 2025, North American financial assets accelerated
by 9.2%, outperforming the global rate of 8.6% (Figure

3). Emerging markets grew even faster, led by Eastern
Europe, China and Latin America, all at around 11%.
Western Europe lagged markedly at 4.5%, while Japan
recorded growth of 6.5%.
Adjusting for inflation and population growth does not
fundamentally change the long-term picture: North
American financial wealth has kept pace with global
growth. Population growth alone accounts for only part of
the story – per-capita wealth creation runs 0.8pps below

the aggregate global rate in most regions, North America
included. The exception is Japan, where a shrinking
population actually lifts per-capita growth above the
headline figure (Figure 4).

Sources: Eurostat, national central banks, financial supervisory authorities, financial associations and statistical offices, IMF, LSEG, Allianz Research
Inflation, by contrast, has a much larger effect than
population growth: it erases more than half of nominal
growth globally in real terms. North America is no
exception, with real per-capita wealth growing at 2.4%

a year against a nominal 5.0%. That 2.4% real rate,
however, is exactly in line with the global average and
comfortably ahead of Western Europe‘s 1.2% – only
Japan and China erode less to inflation than North
America does, and even Japan‘s real per-capita growth
(1.4%) is just narrowly quicker than Western Europe‘s,
not America‘s. Compounded over two decades, this
leaves the purchasing power of North American
financial assets up by a factor of 1.6 – matching the

global multiple, and ahead of the 1.3x recorded in both
Western Europe and Japan. China remains the one
region genuinely out of reach on this measure, with real
per-capita wealth compounding at an astounding 11.9%
a year and purchasing power up almost tenfold over the
same 20 years.
Thanks to its enormous asset base, North America
remains the main driver of global financial wealth
growth in nominal terms, real and per-capita
adjustments notwithstanding. It generated 51.4% of the

worldwide increase in financial assets in 2025. Over the
past two decades, it accounted for 47.5% of the total
increase, compared with 20.1% for China and 14.3%
for Western Europe. In global wealth creation, the US
remains clearly in the driver‘s seat.
North America’s recent outperformance in household
financial-asset growth is a relatively new phenomenon.
In the years following the global financial crisis, US
financial assets frequently grew more slowly than the

global average. The shift began around 2017, when
escalating US–China trade tensions heralded the end of
ever-deeper globalization and the increasingly seamless
integration of emerging economies into the global
division of labor.
This changing environment is also visible in the
narrowing growth gap between emerging and
advanced economies (Figure 5). In the decade leading
up to 2017, emerging-market financial assets on average
grew more than 13pps faster per year. In 2024–2025,

their advantage was only around 3pps. Emerging
markets are still growing faster, but no longer fast
enough to sustain the rapid convergence of the early
2000s. In an increasingly fragmented global economy,
the closing of the global wealth gap has clearly lost
momentum.

at that time included Belgium, the UK, Italy and Japan.

### Page 11

Inflation may be well below its post-pandemic peak, but the affordability crisis has not gone away. In the US, 47%² of
registered voters cite the cost of living as the single most important factor in deciding their 2026 midterm vote – a sign
that lower headline inflation has yet to translate into a sustained recovery in household purchasing power.
In fact, the recovery in purchasing power has gone into reverse. After the post-Covid inflation shock, wage growth had

again overtaken inflation, allowing real incomes to recover in both the US and Europe. But in 2026, renewed energy-price
pressures and sticky services inflation have reversed part of that improvement: inflation has risen to around 3.4% in the
US and 3.3% in Europe and could be pushed higher by the energy-price shock triggered by the war in the Middle East.
With nominal labor-income growth failing to keep pace, real labor-income growth has turned negative again for the first

time since the post-pandemic inflation shock.

Figure 8: Headline inflation and cumulative distributional inflation gap between highest- and lowest-income quintiles, US and Europe, in % and pp

The squeeze is also highly unequal. Lower-income households spend a higher share of their budgets on necessities and
therefore have less room to adjust. In Europe, food, housing and energy absorb around 61% of disposable income for
those in the bottom income quintile, compared with 45% for the top. In the US, lower-income households devote around

6pps more of their spending to essential goods. As a result, differences in consumption baskets can translate into an
effective inflation rate around 1-2pps higher for poorer households.
The cumulative price shock continues to bite as lower inflation rates do not undo earlier price increases: US food prices,
for example, remain around 36% above 2019 levels. For households already spending most of their income on essentials,
this makes it harder to recover the purchasing power lost during the inflation shock.

The affordability hangover can extend from household budgets to the ballot box. Historical evidence across 365
elections in 18 advanced economies since 1948 finds that a 10pp positive inflation surprise is associated with a 15%

increase in the vote share of extremist, anti-system and populist parties, with the effect particularly pronounced when
real wages fall.³ Inflation does not determine voting behavior, but sharp and unexpected losses of purchasing power can
amplify political discontent.
Bringing inflation down is therefore not the same as restoring affordability. Broad price subsidies can provide quick
relief but are costly, often poorly targeted and can weaken incentives to adjust consumption. Support should instead

focus on households most exposed to essential-price shocks, for example through targeted transfers or tax relief, while
structural policies tackle the underlying drivers of living costs – particularly housing supply, energy costs and competition
in essential services. Wage and benefit systems should meanwhile avoid turning temporary price shocks into persistent
inflation.
The longer-term challenge is to protect households’ capacity to save. When essentials absorb more income, saving is

often the adjustment margin, turning today’s affordability squeeze into tomorrow’s wealth gap. Strengthening access to
long-term saving, funded pensions and financial participation can help prevent an inflation shock from leaving a lasting
scar on household wealth.
³  See: Federle, J., Mohr, C. and Schularick, M. (2024), “Inflation Surprises and Election Outcomes,” CEPR Discussion Paper No. 19741.

Markets do the heavy lifting
Financial wealth grows through two channels:
households add new savings, and market movements
change the value of existing portfolios. The second

channel works primarily through securities, especially
equities. Portfolio composition therefore determines how
strongly households benefit from rising markets.
In 2025, securities, including shares, bonds and
investment funds, rose by +12.4%, compared with +5.7%
for bank deposits and +5.0% for insurance and pension
assets (Figure 9). Securities thus led the three major asset
classes for the third consecutive year.
With the sustained stock-market boom delivering

substantial gains, the past three years have been
exceptionally rewarding for invested savers. Between
2023 and 2025, securities grew by an average of +12.1%
annually, more than twice as fast as deposits (+5.2%)
and insurance and pension assets (+5.9%). Their growth
advantage persists over the long term, although it is less
pronounced because capital markets also experience
periodic reversals.
Over the past 20 years, securities grew by an average

of +6.9% annually, compared with +6.0% for total
financial assets. The message is clear: capital-market
participation is increasingly decisive for long-term wealth
accumulation.

Deposits Securities Insurance & pensions
Securities are by far the dominant asset class. At the
end of 2025, they accounted for a record 46.9% of
global financial assets. Despite periodic stock-market
setbacks, the long-term trend has been clear: overall

holdings of shares, investment funds and other securities
increased by 7.4pps compared with 2005. Conversely,
the share of insurance and pension assets declined by a
similar amount to a record low of 24.8%. Bank deposits
meanwhile increased their share slightly to 26.0% (Figure
10).
These global figures mask large differences between
countries and regions, and thus the extent to which
savers benefit from rising securities prices. North
American savers have the greatest capital-market

exposure, with securities accounting for 60.7% of their
portfolios. In the US, the share is even higher, at 61.4%.
This was not always the case: following the global
financial crisis, North America’s securities share fell
below 46%.

Figure 10: Different portfolios, different fortunes.
Gross financial assets, by asset class in 2025 EUR, in % of total gross financial assets
Sources: Eurostat, national central banks, financial supervisory authorities, financial associations and statistical offices, IMF, LSEG, Allianz Research
Securities also play a disproportionately large role in
Latin America, accounting for 45.8% of financial assets.
These holdings, however, are more likely to include

unlisted equity than in North America. Brazil stands out,
with a securities share of 62.1%. In Asia, by contrast,
bank deposits generally play a much larger role: they
account for 54.6% of financial assets in China and 48.8%
in Japan. However, there are important exceptions. In
Australia, many savers participate indirectly in capital
markets through superannuation funds, with insurance
and pension assets accounting for 54.6% of household
portfolios.

Western Europe, meanwhile, is characterized by a more
balanced portfolio structure, with securities significantly
less important than in North America. Here, too, national
differences are substantial: securities account for 54.0%
of financial assets in Italy, while insurance and pension
assets dominate in the Netherlands, with a share of
50.4%.
These different portfolio structures demonstrate the wide
variation in savings behavior around the world. They also

help explain why North American savers have increased
their financial assets broadly in line with global
developments in recent years, unlike their Japanese
and European counterparts. Despite North America’s
already very high level of wealth, its greater exposure
to capital markets has supported continued growth.
Portfolio structure is therefore not merely a reflection of
savings preferences, rather it is an increasingly important
determinant of wealth creation.

Savings losing steam
Savers added less new money in 2025, but markets more
than compensated: 4 out of every 5 euros of additional
financial wealth came from valuation gains rather than

fresh savings.
In 2025, new savings declined by -5.4% to EUR4.1trn
(Figure 11). While this was well below the peaks of the
exceptional years 2020 and 2021, it was still 56% higher
than in 2015. Developments diverged markedly across
regions. New savings in North America fell by -17.5%,
largely because US purchases of securities, insurance
and pension products declined. Nevertheless, the region
still mobilized EUR1.8trn and accounted for around 45%

of total new savings. By contrast, new savings increased
by +34.6% in Japan, +22.2% in Australia and +13.3% in the
eurozone. Overall these flows explain only around one-
fifth of the increase in global assets, with valuation gains
generating the remaining four-fifths.

### Page 15

29 September  2026
15
Another striking feature was the continued recovery in
bank deposits. The savings pool shrank, but the appetite
for liquidity grew: almost one-third of fresh savings
flowed into bank deposits. Inflows had virtually dried up
in 2023, primarily because American savers withdrew
more than EUR500bn and shifted funds into higher-
yielding assets. After recovering in 2024, global deposit
inflows increased by a further +18.1% to EUR1.3trn

in 2025. As a result, deposits attracted 31.7% of new
savings, up from 25.4% in the previous year.
While this is significantly higher than in the previous
two years, it is still significantly lower than in the pre-
pandemic years, when this figure averaged over 40%.
The “rediscovery” of bank deposits is therefore likely due
more to falling interest rates making the opportunity cost
of leaving money in a current account lower, rather than
due to their attractiveness. This is also evident in Japan,

where the delayed interest rate turnaround caused the
proportion of savings in bank deposits to fall to a historic
low of 25%. The recovery was particularly pronounced
in the US, where deposit inflows more than tripled to
EUR321bn. Japan moved in the opposite direction:
although deposit inflows increased slightly in absolute
terms, their share of new savings fell from 28.4% to
23.4%.
Securities suffered another setback after the exceptional
inflows of previous years. Net purchases fell by -20.5% to

EUR1.8trn in 2025, following a decline of -10.9% in 2024.
Even so, securities remained the preferred destination
for new savings, accounting for 42.4% of total flows – a
historically high level – more than any other asset class.
North American savers continued to dominate securities
purchases, but their share fell from almost three-quarters
in 2024 to around two-thirds in 2025. The US alone
accounted for 59% of global purchases. The cooling
of securities flows was therefore largely an American

phenomenon: North American purchases declined by
-29.4% to EUR1.2trn, while inflows remained broadly
stable in Western Europe at EUR333bn and increased
in Japan to EUR79bn. Eastern Europe also recorded a
strong increase, albeit from a much lower base. Global
appetite for securities therefore did not disappear; it
became less concentrated in the US.
Preferences within the securities category shifted again:
households were net sellers of bonds and other debt

securities, with global outflows of EUR63bn compared
with purchases of EUR183bn in 2024. This reversal was
driven by American savers, who sold EUR145bn after
the bond-buying boom of 2022 and 2023. Italian and
Japanese households remained the largest net buyers
among the countries covered, purchasing EUR24bn and
EUR31bn, respectively. However, their paths diverged:
Japanese purchases edged up from EUR29bn in 2024,
while Italian purchases more than halved from EUR55bn.

Shares meanwhile remained in strong demand. Global
purchases reached EUR830bn in 2025, down by -11.3%
from the previous year but still the fourth-highest
amount on record. Once again, the US was the decisive
force, accounting for EUR814bn and therefore virtually
all global net purchases. Elsewhere, activity was broadly
balanced: Western European purchases were close
to zero, while Japanese savers remained net sellers.
Investment funds attracted EUR954bn, a decline of

-11.1% but still the third-highest amount on record. Unlike
direct share purchases, fund inflows were more broadly
distributed: Western European purchases rose by +10.7%
to a record EUR298bn, while North American inflows
fell by -25.0% to EUR500bn. The continued popularity
of Exchange Traded Funds (ETFs) is likely to have
supported these flows.
Inflows into insurance and pension assets declined
by -10.1% to EUR928bn, accounting for only 22.4% of
new savings. However, the global decline was entirely

driven by North America, where inflows fell by -40.8%
to EUR269bn. Western European inflows meanwhile
increased by +25.4% to EUR417bn, while Japan recorded
an even stronger recovery, with inflows rising by +151.9%
to EUR49bn, albeit from a low base. Nevertheless, the
long-term trend remains clear: during the decade before
the pandemic, insurance and pensions attracted an
average of 44% of new savings – almost twice their share
in 2025.

North American savers continue to show a clear
preference for securities. In 2025, they directed 63% of

fresh savings into securities, compared with just 26% in
Western Europe (Figure 12). This greater capital-market
exposure has amplified the effect of their savings, which
becomes visible over the course of ten years.
Within this time period, North American financial assets
grew by an average of +6.9% annually, compared with
+3.9% in Western Europe. This happened although
Western European households added fresh savings
equivalent to an average of 2.4% of their existing

financial assets each year, while North Americans added
just 2.1%.
The decisive difference, however, stems from
contributions generated by valuation gains: they
generated 71% of the increase in North American
financial assets, but only 36% in Western Europe. In other
words, North American portfolios made each euro of
fresh savings work harder.
Germany provides an instructive example. Its financial
assets grew by a relatively strong +6.0% annually

over the past decade, but this required fresh savings
equivalent to 3.9% of existing assets each year –
almost twice the North American rate. Valuation gains
accounted for only 33% of German asset growth, less
than half the North American share. Germany achieved
similar growth through much greater savings efforts;
North America benefited from a substantially stronger
market multiplier.
For Europe, the conclusion is straightforward: its
households do not suffer from a lack of savings, but from

a weaker transmission of savings into wealth creation.
Greater capital-market participation could raise long-
term returns without requiring households to save more.
The question is therefore not only how much Europeans
save, but how effectively their savings are put to work.

Box 2: Who will capture the AI wealth dividend?
Artificial intelligence could transform both the pace and distribution of wealth creation, potentially weakening the
traditional link between working, earning and accumulation. Developing AI models and building out computing, data
and energy infrastructure require large upfront investments, while successful applications can be replicated at low
marginal cost. This favors scale and allows output to grow without a corresponding increase in employment. AI could

also make workers more productive and efficient, raise wages and lower prices. How widely these gains are shared will
depend on policy choices that shape competition, workers’ bargaining power and access to capital ownership.
The shift in value creation from labor towards capital is not new, but AI could accelerate it sharply. Recent US data show
the distributional landscape into which AI is arriving. Pre-tax corporate profits reached an annualized USD4.8trn in the

second quarter of 2026, equivalent to 18% of national income and the highest share since the aftermath of WWII (figure
13). Meanwhile employee compensation fell to 60%, its lowest share since the 1950s. Inflation, outsourcing and cyclical
factors also matter, so these developments cannot be attributed primarily to AI, but they reveal an existing shift towards
capital that AI could amplify if productivity gains accrue mainly through margins, rather than wages.

### Page 18

Allianz Research
18
Figure 13: Profits gain, labor loses ground
US corporate profits and employee compensation, as % of national income
Source: U.S. Bureau of Economic Analysis via FRED
